He had been dining at Marie Léonie’s, she standing, her hands folded before her, hanging down, watching him eat his mutton chops and boiled potatoes, when the porter from his Club had phoned through that there was a wire for him. Marie Léonie had answered the telephone. He had told her to tell the porter to open the telegram and read it to her. That was a not unusual proceeding. Telegrams that came to him at the Club usually announced the results of races that he had not attended. He hated to get up from the dinner-table. She had come back slowly, and said still more slowly that she had bad news for him; there had been an accident; his father had been found shot dead.

He had sat still for quite a time; Marie Léonie also had said nothing. He remembered that he had finished his chops, but had not eaten his apple-pie. He had finished his claret.

===Chinese===
那是桩邪门事。他记得得到消息的时候。他正在玛丽·莱昂妮那里吃晚饭。……

少年的嗓音，恰恰正在为那位女士践踏草地的事道歉。与此同时，德布雷·佩普夫人正说着败坏玛丽·安托瓦内特名誉的话，她显然不喜欢此人。他想不出为什么会有人不喜欢玛丽·安托瓦内特。不过她很可能招人厌。法国人是明白人，把她的脑袋砍了，可见*他们*是不喜欢她的。……

他正在玛丽·莱昂妮那里吃晚饭，她站着，双手交叠垂在身前，看他吃羊排和煮土豆。这时他俱乐部的门房来电话，说有他一封电报。玛丽·莱昂妮接的电话。他叫她告诉门房把电报拆开念给她听。这不算什么稀罕事。送到俱乐部给他的电报，通常是通报他没去成的赛马的结果。他讨厌离开饭桌。她慢慢地走回来，用更慢的声调说，她有个坏消息告诉他；出了事故；他父亲被发现开枪死了。

他坐了好一会儿没动；玛丽·莱昂妮也没说话。他记得他吃完了羊排，但没有吃那块苹果派。红葡萄酒倒是喝完了。

===Original===
By that time he had come to the conclusion that his father had probably committed suicide, and that he﻿—he, Mark Tietjens﻿—was probably responsible for his father’s having done that. He had got up, then, told Marie Léonie to get herself some mourning, and had taken the night train to Groby. There had been no doubt about it when he got there. His father had committed suicide. His father was not the man unadvisedly to crawl through a quicken-hedge with his gun at full-cock behind him, after rabbits.﻿ ﻿… It had been purposed.

There was, then, something soft about the Tietjens stock﻿—for there had been no real and sufficient cause for the suicide. Obviously his father had had griefs. He had never got over the death of his second wife; that was soft for a Yorkshireman. He had lost two sons and an only daughter in the war; other men had done that and got over it. He had heard through him, Mark, that his youngest son﻿—Christopher﻿—was a bad hat. But plenty of men had sons who were bad hats.﻿ ﻿… Something soft then about the stock! Christopher certainly was soft. But that came from the mother. Mark’s stepmother had been from the south of Yorkshire. Soft people down there! a soft woman. Christopher had been her ewe-lamb and she had died of grief when Sylvia had run away from him!﻿ ﻿…

The boy with a voice had got himself into view towards the bottom of the bed, near Mrs. de Bray Pape﻿ ﻿… a tallish slip of a boy, with slightly chawbacony cheeks, high-coloured, lightish hair, brown eyes. Upstanding but softish. Mark seemed to know him, but could not place him. The boy asked to be forgiven for the intrusion, saying that he knew it was not the thing.

===Chinese===
到那时他已经得出结论：他父亲多半是自尽，而他——他，马克·蒂金斯——多半要对父亲之自尽负责。于是他起了身，叫玛丽·莱昂妮去给自己置办丧服，连夜搭上了去格罗比的火车。他到了那里以后，再无疑问。他父亲是自尽。他父亲绝不是那种人会轻率地扳着满机头的枪、把枪别在身后，钻山楂活篱去追兔子。……那是蓄意的。

如此说来，蒂金斯家的种里掺着软的东西——因为那自尽并无真正说得过去的缘由。他父亲显然有伤心事。他始终没走出续弦之死的阴影；对约克郡人来说这就是软。他在战争中失去了两个儿子和一个独女；别人家也遭过这个，也熬过来了。他还从他——马克——嘴里听说，他的小儿子克里斯托弗是个浪荡坯子。可儿子是浪荡坯子的人多着呢。……这么说这血统里果然有软的东西！克里斯托弗确实是软的。但那是随了母亲。马克的继母是约克郡南部人。那一带尽出软人！一个软女人。克里斯托弗是她的心头肉，西尔维娅跟人跑了，她竟伤心而死！……

那个带嗓子的少年挪到了床尾一带，进了他的视野，站在德布雷·佩普夫人近旁……一个细条条的少年，个头不矮，脸颊微微透着乡土气，红扑扑的，头发浅色，眼睛棕色。身板挺括，却有些软。马克似乎认得他，却想不起是哪家的。少年请求原谅他们的唐突，说他知道这不合规矩。

===Original===
Mrs. de Bray Pape was talking improbably about Marie Antoinette, whom she very decidedly disliked. She said that Marie Antoinette had behaved with great ingratitude to Madame de Maintenon﻿—which must have been difficult. Apparently, according to Mrs. de Bray Pape, when Marie Antoinette had been a neglected little girl about the Court of France, Madame de Maintenon had befriended her, lending her frocks, jewels and perfumes. Later Marie Antoinette had persecuted her benefactor. From that had arisen all the woes of France and the Old World in general.

That appeared to Mark to be to mix history, but he was not very certain. Mrs. de Bray Pape said, however, that she had those little-known facts from Mr. Reginald Weiler, the celebrated professor of social economy at one of the Western Universities.

Mark returned to the consideration of the softness of the Tietjens stock, whilst the boy gazed at him with eyes that might have been imploring or that might have been merely moonstruck. Mark could not see what the boy could have to be imploring about, so it was probably just stupidity. His breeches, however, were very nicely cut. Very nicely, indeed; Mark recognized, indeed, the tailor﻿—a man in Conduit Street. If that fellow had the sense to get his riding breeches from that man, he could not be quite an ass.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
德布雷·佩普夫人正满嘴跑火车似的谈着玛丽·安托瓦内特，她对这位王后分明厌恶得很。她说玛丽·安托瓦内特对曼特农夫人的所作所为忘恩负义至极——这想必颇不容易。按德布雷·佩普夫人的说法，当年玛丽·安托瓦内特还是法国宫廷里没人疼的小姑娘时，曼特农夫人善待过她，借给她裙袍、珠宝和香水。后来玛丽·安托瓦内特便迫害起这位恩人来。法兰西乃至整个旧世界的一切灾殃，皆由此而起。

在马克看来这像是把历史搅成了一锅粥，但他不很有把握。不过德布雷·佩普夫人说，这些鲜为人知的事实，她得自雷金纳德·韦勒先生（Mr. Reginald Weiler），西部某所大学赫赫有名的社会经济学教授。

马克回头继续琢磨蒂金斯血统里那点软，那少年则直勾勾望着他，那眼神可能是哀恳，也可能只是犯了痴。马克看不出这少年有什么可哀恳的，所以多半只是痴傻。不过他的马裤剪裁得非常考究。实在考究；马克认出了那位裁缝——康迪特街（Conduit Street）的一家人。这家伙既然懂得去那家做骑马裤，总不至于全然是头蠢驴。……

===Original===
That Christopher was soft because his mother did not come from the north of Yorkshire or Durham might be true enough﻿—but that was not enough to account for the race dying out. His, Mark’s, father had no descendants by his sons. The two brothers who had been killed had been childless. He himself had none. Christopher﻿ ﻿… Well, that was debateable!

That he, Mark, had practically killed his own father he was ready to acknowledge. One made mistakes; that was one. If one made mistakes, one should try to repair them; otherwise, one must, as it were, cut one’s losses. He could not bring his father back to life; he hadn’t, equally, been able to do anything for Christopher.﻿ ﻿… Not much, certainly. The fellow had refused his brass.﻿ ﻿… He couldn’t really blame him.

The boy was asking him if he would not speak to them. He said he was Mark’s nephew, Mark Tietjens junior.

Mark took credit to himself because he did not stir a hair. He had so made up his mind, he found, that Christopher’s son was not his son that he had almost forgotten the cub’s existence. But he ought not to have made up his mind so quickly: he was astonished to find from the automatic working of his mind that he so had. There were too many factors to be considered that he had never bothered really to consider. Christopher had determined that this boy should have Groby: that had been enough for him, Mark. He did not care who had Groby.

===Chinese===
说克里斯托弗软是因为他母亲不是约克郡北部或达勒姆人，也许不假——可这还不足以解释这一族怎么就后继无人。他的——马克的——父亲没能从儿子们身上得到后嗣。阵亡的那两个兄弟没有留下子女。他自己也没有。克里斯托弗……唔，这可就有得争了！

他，马克，等于是亲手害死了自己的父亲，这一点他爽快承认。人总要办错事；这便是一件。办了错事，就该设法补救；不然，就得认赔斩仓。他没法让他父亲还阳；同样，他也一直没能为克里斯托弗做什么。……确切说，没做成什么。那家伙把他的钱推了回来。……说实在的，也不能怪他。

少年问他，肯不肯同他们说话。他说他是马克的侄子，小马克·蒂金斯。

马克因自己毫毛未动而暗自嘉许。他发现自己早已认定克里斯托弗的这个儿子不是他的儿子，认定到几乎忘了这小崽子的存在。可他不该这么快就下定论：从自己头脑自动运转的情形看，他吃惊地发现确实如此。有太多该斟酌的因素，他从来没有认真斟酌过。克里斯托弗拿定主意要把格罗比给这孩子：对他，对马克来说，有这一条就够了。他才不管谁得格罗比。

===Original===
But the actual sight of this lad whom he had never seen before presented the problem to him as something that needed solution. It came as a challenge. When he came to think of it, it was a challenge to him to make up his mind finally as to the nature of Woman. He imagined that he had never bothered his head about that branch of the animal kingdom. But he found that, lying there, he must have spent quite a disproportionate amount of his time in thinking about the motives of Sylvia.

He had never spoken much with any but men﻿—and then mostly with men of his own class and type. Naturally you addressed a few polite words to your weekend hostess. If you found yourself in the rose-garden of a Sunday before church with a young or old woman who knew anything about horses, you talked about horses, or Goodwood, or Ascot to her for long enough to show politeness to your hostess’s guests. If she knew nothing about horses you talked about the roses or the irises, or the weather last week. But that pretty well exhausted it.

Nevertheless, he knew all about women. Of that he was confident. That is to say, that when in the course of conversation or gossip he had heard the actions of women narrated or commented on, he had always been able to supply a motive for those actions sufficient to account for them to his satisfaction, or to let him predict with accuracy what course the future would take. No doubt, twenty years of listening to the almost ceaseless but never disagreeable conversation of Marie Léonie had been a liberal education.

===Chinese===
可这个他从未谋面的少年此刻真真切切立在眼前，便把这道难题摆成了非解不可的样子。它像一纸挑战书。细想起来，这挑战是逼他最终拿定主意，判定女人这一支动物的天性究竟如何。他自以为从没为动物界这一支伤过脑筋。可他发现，自己躺在那儿，准是把相当不成比例的时间，花在了琢磨西尔维娅的动机上。

他平素说话的对象几乎只有男人——而且多是跟他同一阶级、同一路数的男人。当然，对周末做客那家的女主人，你总得寒暄几句。若星期天晨祷前，你在玫瑰园里同一位懂马的女士——年轻的老的都一样——不期而遇，你就同她谈马，谈古德伍德或阿斯科特，谈上一阵，礼数就算到了。她若不懂马，你就谈玫瑰或鸢尾，或谈谈上礼拜的天气。如此而已。

尽管如此，他对女人无所不知。这一点他有把握。也就是说，每当闲谈或嚼舌时听到人们讲起或评点女人家的行径，他总能给那些行径安上一个足以自圆其说的动机，让自己满意，或者借此精准料定后事将如何发展。毫无疑问，二十年来听玛丽·莱昂妮那几乎永不间断、却从不叫人厌烦的谈话，等于受了一场充分的通才教育。

===Original===
He regarded his association with her with complete satisfaction﻿—as the only subject for complete satisfaction to be found in the contemplation of the Tietjens family. Christopher’s Valentine was a pretty piece enough and had her head screwed confoundedly well on. But Christopher’s association with her had brought such a peck of troubles down on his head that, except for the girl as an individual, it was a pretty poor choice. It was a man’s job to pick a woman who would neither worry him nor be the cause of worries. Well, Christopher had picked two﻿—and look at the results!

He himself had been completely unmistaken﻿—from the first minute. He had first seen Marie Léonie on the stage at Covent Garden. He had gone to Covent Garden in attendance on his stepmother, his father’s second wife﻿—the soft woman. A florid, gentle, really saintly person. She had passed around Groby for a saint. An Anglican saint, of course. That was what was the matter with Christopher. It was the soft streak. A Tietjens had no business with saintliness in his composition! It was bound to get him looked on as a blackguard!

But he had attended Covent Garden as a politeness to his stepmother, who very seldom found herself in Town. And there, in the second row of the ballet, he had seen Marie Léonie﻿—slimmer, of course, in those days. He had at once made up his mind to take up with her, and, an obliging commissionaire having obtained her address for him from the stage-door, he had, towards twelve-thirty next day, walked along the Edgware Road towards her lodgings. He had intended to call on her; he met her, however, in the street. Seeing her there, he had liked her walk, her figure, her neat dress.

===Chinese===
他看待自己同她的结合，怀着全然的满足——检点蒂金斯全家，唯有这一桩能让人全然满足。克里斯托弗的瓦伦丁够标致，脑子也出奇地清醒。但克里斯托弗同她的结合给他惹来一大堆麻烦，撇开姑娘本人不论，这选择实在够糟的。挑一个既不烦扰你、也不成为祸根的女人，是男人分内的本事。结果呢，克里斯托弗挑了两个——瞧瞧这下场！

他自己则从头一分钟就没看错人。他初见玛丽·莱昂妮，是在考文特花园的舞台上。他去考文特花园，是为陪伴继母——父亲的第二任妻子——那个软女人。一个红光满面、温温柔柔、真正圣洁的人。她在格罗比一带被公认为圣徒。当然，是英国国教的圣徒。克里斯托弗的毛病正出在这里。就是那股软筋。蒂金斯家的人体质里掺不得圣徒气质！掺了就注定被人当成恶棍！

那回他上考文特花园，是对继母尽一份礼数——她难得进一趟城。就在那里，芭蕾的第二排上，他看见了玛丽·莱昂妮——那时候自然更苗条。他当即拿定主意要同她好。一位热心肠的门房替他从后台要来了她的住址，于是第二天晌午十二点半前后，他沿着埃奇韦尔路朝她的寓所走去。他本打算登门拜访；却在街上迎面遇见了她。在那儿一眼望去，他就喜欢上了她的步态、她的身段、她齐整的衣裳。

===Original===
He had planted himself, his umbrella, his billycock hat and all, squarely in front of her﻿—she had neither flinched nor attempted to bolt round him﻿—and had said that, if at the end of her engagement in London, she cared to be placed “*dans ses draps*,” with two hundred and fifty pounds a year and pin money to be deliberated on, she might hang up her cream-jug at an apartment that he would take for her in St. John’s Wood Park, which was the place in which, in those days, most of his friends had establishments. She had preferred the neighbourhood of the Gray’s Inn Road, as reminding her more of France.

But Sylvia was quite another pair of shoes.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
他把自己连人带伞、连圆顶毡帽，端端正正地杵在她面前——她既没缩，也没想从他身边绕开——然后说，倘若等她在伦敦的演出合同期满之后，她愿意被「安顿进他的被窝」（*dans ses draps*），年俸二百五十镑，零用另议，那么她尽可以把她的奶油罐挂到一套公寓里去——他会在圣约翰伍德公园替她租下；那些年，他的朋友们大多把外宅安在那地方。她却更喜欢格雷律师学院街一带，说那更让她想起法国。

可西尔维娅完全是另一码事。……

===Original===
That young man was flushing all over his face. The young of the tomtit in the old shoe were getting impatient; they were chirruping in spite of the alarm-cries of the mother on the boughs above the thatch. It was certainly insanitary to have boughs above your thatch, but what did it matter in days so degenerate that even the young of tomtits could not restrain their chirpings in face of their appetites.

That young man﻿—Sylvia’s by-blow﻿—was addressing embarrassed remarks to Mrs. de Bray Pape. He suggested that perhaps his uncle resented the lady’s lectures on history and sociology. He said they had come to talk about the tree. Perhaps that was why his uncle would not speak to them.

The lady said that it was precisely giving lessons in history to the dissolute aristocracy of the Old World that was her mission in life. It was for their good, resent it how they might. As for talking about the tree, the young man had better do it for himself. She now intended to walk around the garden to see how the poor lived.

===Chinese===
那少年满脸涨得通红。旧鞋里的山雀雏鸟们等得不耐烦了；尽管茅檐上枝头里的母雀连连发着警报，它们还是唧唧啾啾叫个不停。屋顶茅草上头压着树枝，确实有碍卫生；可这年月连山雀的雏鸟都管不住嘴，一见了吃食就叫，卫生不卫生又算得了什么。

那少年——西尔维娅的私生种——正向德布雷·佩普夫人讪讪地说着话。他委婉表示，伯父也许是不耐烦听这位女士讲历史和社会学。他说他们是来谈那棵树的。也许伯父不肯说话，为的就是这个。

女士说，给旧世界淫荡的贵族上历史课，正是她此生的使命。这是为他们好，随他们怎么怀恨。至于谈树，少年最好自己去谈。她现在打算到园子里转转，看看穷人是怎么过活的。

===Original===
The boy said that in that case he did not see why Mrs. de Bray Pape had come at all. The lady answered that she had come at the sacred behest of his injured mother. That ought to be answer enough for him. She flitted, disturbedly from Mark’s view.

The boy, swallowing visibly in his throat, fixed his slightly protruding eyes on his uncle’s face. He was about to speak, but he remained for a long time silent and goggling. That was a Christopher Tietjens trick﻿—not a Tietjens family trick. To gaze at you a long time before speaking. Christopher had it, no doubt, from his mother﻿—exaggeratedly. She would gaze at you for a long time. Not unpleasantly, of course. But Christopher had always irritated him, even as a small boy.﻿ ﻿… It is possible that he himself might not be as he was if he hadn’t gazed at him for a long time, like a stuck pig. On the morning of that beastly day. Armistice Day.﻿ ﻿… Beastly.

Cramp’s eldest son, a bugler in the second Hampshires, went down the path, his bugle shining behind his khaki figure. Now they would make a beastly row with that instrument. On Armistice Day they had played the Last Post on the steps of the church under Marie Léonie’s windows.﻿ ﻿… The Last Post!﻿ ﻿… The Last of England! He remembered thinking that. He had not by then had the full terms of that surrender, but he had had a dose enough of Christopher’s stuck-piggedness!﻿ ﻿… A full dose! He didn’t say he didn’t deserve it. If you make mistakes you must take what you get for it. You shouldn’t make mistakes.

===Chinese===
少年说，那样的话，他不明白德布雷·佩普夫人到底来干什么。女士答道，她是奉他那位受了伤害的母亲的神圣差遣而来。这话对他该是足够的回答了。她心绪不宁地从马克的视野里翩然而去。

少年喉结一上一下地咽着口水，把他那双微微鼓出的眼睛定在伯父脸上。他欲言又止，半晌只是干瞪着。这是克里斯托弗·蒂金斯的做派——不是蒂金斯家传的做派。开口之前先盯着你瞅上半天。克里斯托弗这毛病无疑得自他母亲——还放大了三倍。她就会那么盯着你瞅半天。倒不至于叫人不快。可克里斯托弗从小就惹他烦，还是个小不点儿的时候就是。……倘若不是他那么像一头被扎的猪似的盯着自己瞅了那么久，他自己也许未必是如今这副样子。在那个可憎的日子的早晨。停战日。……可憎。

克兰普家的大儿子——第二汉普郡团的号手——顺着小径走下去，军号在他卡其色的身影背后闪闪发亮。这下他们要拿那玩意儿奏出可憎的噪音了。停战日那天，他们就是在玛丽·莱昂妮窗下的教堂台阶上吹的熄灯号（the Last Post）。……熄灯号！……最后的英格兰！他记得自己当时就这么想。那时他还没拿到那份投降书的全部条款，可克里斯托弗那副被扎的猪似的瞪眼相，他领教的一剂已经够足！……足足了剂！他倒不是说这不该他受。办了错事，就得自食其果。本不该办错事。

===Original===
The boy at the foot of the bed was making agonized motions with his throat: swallowing his Adam’s apple.

He said:

“I can understand, uncle, that you hate to see us. All the same, it seems a little severe to refuse to speak to us!”

===Chinese===
床脚的少年正做着痛苦的喉部动作：一口一口吞着他的喉结。

他说：

「伯父，我能理解，您见到我们心里难受。可怎么着也不肯同我们说话，未免太严厉了！」

===Original===
Mark wondered a little at the breakdown in communications that there must have been. Sylvia had been spying round that property, and round and round and round again. She had had renewed interviews with Mrs. Cramp. It had struck him as curious taste to like to reveal to dependents﻿—to reveal and to dwell upon the fact that you were distasteful to your husband. If his woman had left him he would have preferred to hold his tongue about it. He certainly would not have gone caterwauling about it to the carpenter of the man she had taken up with. Still, there was no accounting for tastes. Sylvia had, no doubt, been so full of her own griefs that very likely she had not listened to what Mrs. Cramp had said about his, Mark’s, condition. On the one or two interviews he had had with that bitch she had been like that. She had sailed in with her grievances against Christopher with such vigour that she had gone away with no ideas at all as to the conditions on which she was to be allowed to inhabit Groby. Obviously it taxed her mind to invent what she invented. You could not invent that sort of sex-cruelty stuff without having your mind a little affected. She could not, for instance, have invented the tale that he, Mark, was suffering for the sins of his youth without its taking it out of her. That is the ultimate retribution of Providence on those who invent gossip frequently. They go a little dotty.﻿ ﻿… The fellow﻿—he could not call his name to mind, half Scotch, half Jew, who had told him the worst tales against Christopher had gone a little dotty. He had grown a beard and wore a top-hat at inappropriate functions. Well, in effect, Christopher was a saint, and Provvy invents retributions of an ingenious kind against those who libel saints.

At any rate, that bitch must have become so engrossed in her tale that it had not come through to her that he, Mark, could not speak. Of course, the results of venereal disease are not pleasant to contemplate, and, no doubt, Sylvia, having invented the disease for him, had not liked to contemplate the resultant symptoms. At any rate, that boy did not know﻿—and neither did Mrs. de Bray Pape﻿—that he did not speak. Not to them, not to anybody. He was finished with the world. He perceived the trend of its actions, listened to its aspirations, and even to its prayers, but he would never again stir lip or finger. It was like being dead﻿—or being God.

This boy was apparently asking for absolution. He was of opinion that it was not a very sporting thing of himself and Mrs. Bray to come there.﻿ ﻿…

It was, however, sporting enough. He could see that they were both as afraid of him, Mark, as of the very devil. Its taste might, however, be questioned. Still, the situation was unusual﻿—as all situations are. Obviously it was not in good taste for a boy to come to the house in which his father lived with a mistress, nor for the wife’s intimate friend either. Still they apparently wanted, the one to let, the other to take, Groby. They could not do either if he, Mark, did not give permission, or, at any rate, if he opposed them. It was business, and business may be presumed to cover quite a lot of bad taste.

===Chinese===
马克有点纳闷，这里头的传话链条究竟是哪一环断了。西尔维娅一直在那处产业周围刺探，转了一圈又一圈又一圈。她同克兰普太太又约谈过几回。他总觉得这口味古怪：爱向下人吐露——不但吐露，还要细细咀嚼自己如何讨丈夫嫌这件事。他的女人若离开了他，他宁可守口如瓶。他断然不会跑到她搭上的男人手下的木匠家里，把这事嚎得满村都是。不过，人各有好，没法子。西尔维娅无疑满脑子都是自己的委屈，多半压根没听克兰普太太讲他——马克的——病况。他同那母狗打过一两回交道，她就是这副样子。她带着满帆的怨气冲进来，劲头那么足，以致走的时候，对她获准住进格罗比的条件一个字也没弄清。显然，编造她那些发明太费脑子。编那一套性虐待的货色，脑子不先坏掉几分是编不出来的。比方说，她编出他马克是年轻时造孽遭的报应这套话，就不可能不掏空她自己的心力。这便是老天爷对惯造是非之人的终极报应：他们会变得有点疯疯癫癫。……那个家伙——名字他想不起来了，半苏格兰半犹太的那个，对他讲过克里斯托弗最坏的话的——就变得有点疯疯癫癫。他留了胡子，还在不相干的场合戴大礼帽。可不是嘛，克里斯托弗事实上是个圣徒，而老天爷专会造出精巧的报应，加在诽谤圣徒的人身上。

无论如何，那母狗准是讲她的故事讲得太入神，竟没有传到她耳朵里：他，马克，是不能说话的。当然，性病的恶果想起来不愉快，西尔维娅既然替他发明了这病，多半也不愿去想那些随之而来的症状。总之，那少年不知道——德布雷·佩普夫人也不知道——他不开口。不同他们说话，也不同任何人说话。他同这个世界已经两讫了。他察看它的行动走向，听它的宏愿，甚至听它的祷告，但他再也不会动一动嘴唇或手指。这就像死了——或者像当了上帝。

这少年显然是在请求赦罪。他自己的看法是，他和佩普夫人跑到这里来，不算十分光明磊落。……

不过，也算得上光明磊落了。他看得出，他们俩对他——对马克——怕得要死，像怕魔鬼一样。只是这事的品味大可商榷。当然，局面本就不寻常——天下的局面哪个寻常。一个少年跑到父亲与外室同居的宅子里来，做妻子的密友也跑来，显然都有失体统。可他们一个显然要出租格罗比，一个要租下。他马克若不点头——至少若从中作梗——他们哪头也办不成。这是生意；而生意这东西，想必容得下相当多的失体统。

===Original===
And, in effect, the boy was saying that his mother was, of course, a splendid person, but that he, Mark Junior, found her proceedings in many respects questionable. One could not, however, expect a woman﻿—and an injured woman﻿ ﻿… The boy, with his shining eyes and bright cheeks, seemed to beg Mark to concede that his mother was at least an injured woman.﻿ ﻿… One could not expect, then, a wronged woman to see things eye to eye with﻿ ﻿… with young Cambridge! For, he hastened to assure Mark, his Set﻿—the son of the Prime Minister, young Doble, and Porter, as well as himself, were unanimously of opinion that a man ought to be allowed to live with whom he liked. He was not, therefore, questioning his father’s actions, and, for himself, if the occasion arose, he would be very glad to shake his father’s﻿ ﻿… companion﻿ ﻿… by the hand.

His bright eyes became a little humid. He said that he was not in effect questioning anything, but he thought that he himself would have been the better for a little more of his father’s influence. He considered that he had been too much under his mother’s influence. They noticed it, even at Cambridge! That, in effect, was the real snag when it came to be a question of dissolving unions once contracted. Scientifically considered. Questions of﻿ ﻿… of sex attraction, in spite of all the efforts of scientists, remained fairly mysterious. The best way to look at it﻿ ﻿… the safest way, was that sex attraction occurred, as a rule, between temperamental and physical opposites, because Nature desired to correct extremes. No one, in fact, could be more different than his father and mother﻿—the one so graceful, athletic and﻿ ﻿… oh, charming. And the other so﻿ ﻿… oh, let us say perfectly honourable, but﻿ ﻿… oh, lawless. Because, of course, you can break certain laws and remain the soul of honour.

Mark wondered if this boy was aware that his mother habitually informed everyone whom she met that his father lived on women. On the immoral earnings of women, she would infer when she thought it safe.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
果然，少年正说着：他母亲自然是个了不起的人，但他，小马克，觉得她的行事在许多方面不无可疑之处。不过，谁也不能指望一个女人——一个受了伤害的女人……这少年眼睛晶亮、双颊绯红，一副恳求马克承认他母亲至少是个受害者的模样。……那么，谁也不能指望一个受害的女人同……同年轻的剑桥看法一致！因为他赶紧向马克保证，他们那个圈子——首相的公子小多布尔、波特，连他自己在内——一致认为，一个男人愿意同谁同居，就该被容许同谁同居。因此他并非在质疑父亲的行为；而就他自己而言，倘有机缘，他非常乐意同他父亲的……伴侣……握一握手。

他晶亮的眼睛有点湿润了。他说，实际上他什么也没有质疑，只是觉得，父亲的影响若能多得几分，他自己会受益更多。他认为自己受母亲的影响太深了。这一点他们都察觉了，连剑桥都察觉了！而这，实际上正是解除既成婚姻时真正的症结。从科学上考虑。性……性吸引的问题，尽管科学家们百般努力，仍相当神秘。看待它最好的方式……最稳妥的方式，是认为性吸引通常发生在气质与体格相反的两者之间，因为大自然想要矫正极端。事实上，没有谁比他的父母更不相同了——一位那样优雅矫健，噢，迷人。另一位那样……噢，这么说吧，彻头彻尾地光明磊落，可是……噢，无法无天。因为，当然啦，人可以破坏某些法律，而仍不失为荣誉的化身。

马克寻思，这少年可知道，他母亲逢人便说、成了习惯：他父亲靠女人养活。靠女人卖身的钱过活——她觉得稳妥的时候，就往这层意思上引。……

===Original===
The soul of honour, then, and masculinely clumsy and damn fine in his way.﻿ ﻿… Well, he, Mark Tietjens junior, was not there to judge his father. His uncle Mark could see that he regarded his father with affection and admiration. But if Nature﻿—he must be pardoned for using anthropomorphic expressions since they were the shortest way﻿—if Nature, then, meant unions of opposite characters to redress extremes in the children, the process did not complete itself with﻿ ﻿… in short, with the act of physical union. For just as there were obviously inherited physical characteristics, and, no doubt, inherited memory, there yet remained the question of the influence of temperament by means of personal association. So that for one opposite to leave the fruits of a union exclusively under the personal influence of the other opposite was very possibly to defeat the purposes of Nature﻿ ﻿…

That boy, Mark thought, was a very curious problem. He seemed to be a good, straight boy. A little loquacious; still, that was to be excused since he had to do all the talking himself. From time to time he had paused in his speech as if, deferentially, he wished to have Mark’s opinion. That was proper. He, Mark, could not stand hobbledehoys﻿—particularly the hobbledehoys of that age, who appeared to be opinionative and emotional beyond the normal in hobbledehoys. Anyhow, he could not stand the Young once they were beyond the age of childhood. But he was aware that, if you want to conduct a scientific investigation, if you want to arrive, for yourself, at the truth of an individual’s parentage﻿—you must set aside your likes and dislikes.

===Chinese===
那么，荣誉的化身，而且男人气地笨拙、自有一股该死的好。……好了，他，小马克·蒂金斯，可不是来审判父亲的。伯父马克看得出来，他对父亲怀着爱与敬仰。可倘若大自然——请原谅他使用拟人的说法，因为这样说最省事——倘若大自然安排性格相反者结合，是为了在子女身上矫正极端，那么这一过程并不终结于……简言之，并不终结于肉体结合这个行为本身。因为正如肉体特征显然可以遗传，记忆无疑也可以遗传，这里头还剩下一个问题：气质通过朝夕相处所施加的影响。如此说来，对立的一方若把结合的果实完全置于另一对立一方的个人影响之下，就很可能挫败大自然的目的……

这少年，马克想，是道非常费解的难题。他似乎是个正派直爽的孩子。有点话多；不过可以原谅，毕竟话全得他一个人说。他不时在话中间停一停，仿佛恭恭敬敬地想听听马克的意见。这合规矩。他，马克，受不了半大小子——尤其是那个岁数的半大小子，比寻常的半大小子更武断、更容易感情用事。反正，年轻人一过了孩童的年纪，他一概受不了。但他心里明白：你若要搞一项科学的调查，若要靠自己弄清一个人的亲生父母究竟是谁——就必须把好恶撇在一边。

===Original===
Heaven knew, he had found Christopher, when he had been only one of the younger ones in his father’s﻿—he had found him irritating enough﻿ ﻿… a rather moony, fair brat, interested mostly in mathematics, with a trick of standing with those goggle eyes gazing bluely at you﻿—years ago, in and around, at first the nursery, then the stables at Groby. Then, if this lad irritated him, it was rather an argument in favour of his being Christopher’s son than Sylvia’s by-blow by another man.﻿ ﻿… What was the fellow’s name? A rank bad hat, anyhow.

The probability was that he *was* the other fellow’s son. That woman would not have trepanned Christopher into the marriage if she hadn’t at least thought that she was with child. There was nothing to be said against any wench’s tricking any man into marrying her if she were in that condition. But once having got a man to give a name to your bastard you ought to treat him with some loyalty; it is a biggish service he has done you. That Sylvia had never done.﻿ ﻿… They had got this young fellow into their﻿—the Tietjenses’﻿—family. There he was, with his fingers on Groby already.﻿ ﻿… That was all right. As great families as Tietjens’ had had that happen to them.

But what made Sylvia pestilential was that she should afterwards have developed this sex-madness for his unfortunate brother.

===Chinese===
天知道，当年克里斯托弗还只是他父亲膝下——还只是那群小的里头的一个时，他就觉得这孩子够呛……一个白净、迷瞪的小崽子，一心痴迷数学，动不动就站在那儿，鼓着一双蓝汪汪的眼睛瞅你——那是多年以前的事了，先是在育儿室里外，后来是格罗比的马厩。这么说，倘若这小子也惹他烦，这倒更是一条论据，证明他是克里斯托弗的儿子，而不是西尔维娅跟别的男人生的私种。……那家伙叫什么来着？反正是个不折不扣的浪荡坯。

多半他**就是**那另一个家伙的儿子。那女人若不是至少自以为有了身孕，也不会设套把克里斯托弗诓进婚姻。一个丫头处在那种境地，不管使什么计谋诓哪个男人娶她，都没什么可指摘的。可一旦得了手、让男人给你的私生子冠了姓，你就该待他有些忠义；这可是他帮你办的一件不小的事。这一点西尔维娅从来没做到。……他们把这个少年弄进了他们——蒂金斯家的——门里。他就在这儿，手指头已经搭上格罗比了。……这也罢了。同蒂金斯一样显赫的门第，这种事也不是没发生过。

可恨的是，西尔维娅后来竟对他那位倒霉兄弟生出了这股性的痴狂，这才让她成了瘟神。

===Original===
There was no other way to look at it. She had undoubtedly lured Christopher on to marry her because she thought, rightly or wrongly, that she was with child by another man. They would never know﻿—she herself probably did not know!﻿—whether this boy was Christopher’s son or the other’s. English women are so untidy﻿—shamefaced﻿—about these things. That was excusable. But every other action of hers from that date had been inexcusable﻿—except regarded as actions perpetrated under the impulsion of sex-viciousness.

It is perfectly proper﻿—it is a mother’s duty to give an unborn child a name and a father. But afterwards to blast the name of that father is more discreditable than to leave the child nameless. This boy was now Tietjens of Groby﻿—but he was also the boy who was the son of a father who had behaved unspeakably according to the mother.﻿ ﻿… And the son of a mother who had been unable to attract her man!﻿ ﻿… Who advertised the fact to the estate carpenter! If we say that the good of the breed is the supreme law what sort of virtue was this?

===Chinese===
这事没有别的看法。她无疑是引诱克里斯托弗娶她的，因为她自以为——对也罢错也罢——怀了另一个男人的孩子。他们永远无从知道——她自己也多半不知道！——这少年究竟是克里斯托弗的儿子还是那另一个的。英国女人在这些事上就是这样马虎——这样羞于较真。这还可以原谅。可从那天起，她的每一个举动都无可原谅——除非把它们看作在性恶的驱使下犯下的行径。

这原是完全正当的——给未出生的孩子一个姓氏、一个父亲，是做母亲的义务。可事后再去败坏那父亲的名声，就比索性让孩子没名没姓更下作。这少年如今是格罗比的蒂金斯了——可他同时也是这样一个孩子：按他母亲的说法，有个行为不堪言说的父亲。……还有个拴不住自己男人的母亲！……还把这事实张扬到庄园木匠的老婆耳朵里！倘若我们说种族的利益是至高法则，这算哪门子德行？

===Original===
It was all very well to say that every one of Sylvia’s eccentricities had in view the sole aim of getting her boy’s father to return to her. No doubt they might. He, Mark, was perfectly ready to concede that even her infidelities, notorious as they had been, might have been merely ways of calling his unfortunate brother’s attention back to her﻿—of keeping herself in his mind. After the marriage Christopher, finding out that he had been a mere cat’s-paw, probably treated her pretty coldly or ignored her﻿—maritally.﻿ ﻿… And he was a pretty attractive fellow, Christopher. He, Mark, was bound nowadays to acknowledge that. A regular saint and Christian martyr and all that.﻿ ﻿… Enough to drive a woman wild if she had to live beside him and be ignored.

It is obvious that women must be allowed what means they can make use of to maintain﻿—to arouse﻿—their sex attraction for their men. That is what the bitches are for in the scale of things. They have to perpetuate the breed. To do that they have to call attention to themselves and to use what devices they see fit to use, each one according to her own temperament. That cruelty was an excitant, he was quite ready, too, to concede. He was ready to concede anything to the woman. To be cruel is to draw attention to yourself; you cannot expect to be courted by a man whom you allow to forget you. But there probably ought to be a limit to things. You probably ought in this, as in all other things, to know what you can do and what you can’t﻿—and the proof of this particular pudding, as of all others, was in the eating. Sylvia had left no stone unturned in the determination to keep herself in her man’s mind, and she had certainly irretrievably lost her man: to another girl. Then she was just a nuisance.

A woman intent on getting a man back ought to have some system, some sort of scheme at the very least. But Sylvia﻿—he knew it from the interminable talk that he had had with Christopher on Armistice Night﻿—Sylvia delighted most in doing what she called pulling the strings of shower-baths. She did extravagant things, mostly of a cruel kind, for the fun of seeing what would happen. Well, you cannot allow yourself fun when you are on a campaign. Not as to the subject matter of the campaign itself! If then you do what you want rather than what is expedient, you damn well have to take what you get for it. *Damn* well!

===Chinese===
固然可以这样说：西尔维娅的每一桩乖张行径，唯一的目的都是让孩子的父亲回到她身边。无疑可能是这样。他，马克，完全乐于承认：就连她那些尽人皆知的外遇，也可能不过是唤回他那位倒霉兄弟注意的法子——好让自己留在他心里。婚后克里斯托弗发现自己不过是枚被人利用的猫爪子，多半待她相当冷淡，或在夫妻情分上干脆不理她。……而克里斯托弗这人又是很有吸引力的。这一点，如今他马克不能不承认。地道的圣徒加基督教殉道者，全套家什。……哪个女人守着这么个人过活却又受冷落，不疯才怪。

女人为了维持——为了唤起——她们对男人的性吸引，使出什么手段都该被容许，这是明摆着的。在万物的秩序里，母狗们的用处就在这里。她们得让种族延续。为此她们就得招人注意，各按各的性子，使出各自看家的手段。残忍是一种兴奋剂，这他也十分乐于承认。他乐于向女人承认一切。残忍就是引人注意你自己；你若由着一个男人把你忘掉，就不能指望他来追求你。可事情总该有个限度。在这件事上，同在一切事上一样，你大约应该知道自己什么做得、什么做不得——而这块布丁的好坏，同所有布丁一样，吃了才知道。西尔维娅为把自己留在她男人的心里，可谓掘地三尺机关用尽，而她确凿无疑、无可挽回地失去了她的男人：输给了另一个姑娘。那么她就只剩讨厌了。

一心要夺回男人的女人，总该有套章法，至少该有个大概的谋划。可西尔维娅——这是他停战日之夜同克里斯托弗那场没完没了的长谈里知道的——西尔维娅最喜欢的，是干她所谓的「拉淋浴的绳子」：干出些铺张的事，多半是残忍的一路，就为瞧瞧会溅出什么水花。哼，打仗的时候可由不得你贪玩。更不能拿战事本身当玩闹！既要任性而不权衡利害，那就活该自食其果。**活该**！

===Original===
What would have justified Sylvia, no matter what she did, would have been if she had succeeded in having another child by his brother. She hadn’t. The breed of Tietjens was not enriched. Then she was just a nuisance.﻿ ﻿…

An infernal nuisance.﻿ ﻿… For what was she up to now? It was perfectly obvious that both Mrs. de Bray Pape and this boy were here because she had had another outbreak of﻿ ﻿… practically Sadism. They were here so that Christopher might be hurt some more and she not forgotten. What, then, was it? What the deuce was it?

The boy had been silent for some time. He was gazing at Mark with the goggle-eyed gasping that had been so irritating in his father﻿—particularly on Armistice Day.﻿ ﻿… Well, he, Mark, was apparently now conceding that this boy was probably his brother’s son. A real Tietjens after all was to reign over the enormously long, grey house behind the fantastic cedar. The tallest cedar in Yorkshire. In England. In the Empire.﻿ ﻿… He didn’t care. He who lets a tree overhang his roof calls the doctor in daily.﻿ ﻿… The boy’s lips began to move. No sound came out. He was presumably in a great state!

===Chinese===
无论西尔维娅干了什么，只要她真能再给他兄弟生个孩子，就都情有可原。她没能。蒂金斯的血脉没有添丁。那么她就只剩讨厌了。……

讨厌得邪门。……因为她如今又在算计什么？明明白白：德布雷·佩普夫人和这少年到这里来，是因为她又发作了一回……简直是性虐狂（Sadism）的发作。他们来，为的是让克里斯托弗再多受些伤，让她自己不被忘记。那这算什么？这到底是什么鬼名堂？

少年已经沉默了一会儿。他正瞪着眼睛、屏着气凝视马克，那副样子在他父亲身上曾那么惹人烦——尤其是在停战日那天。……好了，他马克这会儿显然算是认了：这少年多半就是他兄弟的儿子。到底还是有一个真正的蒂金斯，要来君临那株奇伟雪松后面那座长得望不到头的灰房子了。约克郡最高的雪松。英格兰最高的。整个帝国最高的。……他才不在乎。谁让树荫罩住自家的屋顶，谁就是天天把医生往家里请。……少年的嘴唇动了起来。没有声音出来。他想必紧张到了极点！

===Original===
He was undoubtedly like his father. Darker﻿ ﻿… Brown hair, brown eyes, high-coloured cheeks all flushed now. Straight nose; marked brown eyebrows. A sort of﻿ ﻿… scared, puzzled﻿ ﻿… what was it?﻿ ﻿… expression. Well, Sylvia was fair; Christopher was dark-haired with silver streaks, but fair-complexioned.﻿ ﻿… Damn it: this boy was more attractive than Christopher had been at his age and earlier.﻿ ﻿… Christopher hanging round the schoolroom door in Groby, puzzled over the mathematical theory of waves. He, Mark, hadn’t been able to stand him or, indeed, any of the other children. There was sister Effie﻿—*born* to be a curate’s wife.﻿ ﻿… Puzzled! That was it!﻿ ﻿… That bothering woman, his father’s second wife﻿—the Saint!﻿—had introduced the puzzlement strain into the Tietjenses.﻿ ﻿… This was Christopher’s boy, saintly strain and all. Christopher was probably born to be a rural dean in a fat living writing treatises on the integral calculus all the time except on Saturday afternoons. With a great reputation for saintliness. Well, he wasn’t the one and hadn’t the other. He was an old furniture dealer, who made a stink in virtuous nostrils.﻿ ﻿… Provvy works in a mysterious way. The boy was saying now:

“The tree﻿ ﻿… the great tree.﻿ ﻿… It darkens the windows.﻿ ﻿…”

Mark said: “Oha!” to himself. Groby Great Tree was the symbol of Tietjens. For thirty miles round Groby they made their marriage vows by Groby Great Tree. In the other Ridings they said that Groby Tree and Groby well were equal in height and depth one to the other. When they were really imaginatively drunk Cleveland villagers would declare﻿—would knock you down if you denied﻿—that Groby Great Tree was 365 foot high and Groby well 365 feet deep. A foot for every day of the year.﻿ ﻿… On special occasions﻿—he could not himself be bothered to remember what﻿—they would ask permission to hang rags and things from the boughs. Christopher said that one of the chief indictments against Joan of Arc had been that she and the other village girls of Domrémy had hung rags and trinkets from the boughs of a cedar. Offerings to fairies.﻿ ﻿… Christopher set great store by the tree. He was a romantic ass. Probably he set more store by the tree than by anything else at Groby. He would pull the house down if he thought it incommoded the tree.

===Chinese===
他无疑像他父亲。肤色深些……棕色头发，棕色眼睛，红扑扑的脸颊此刻涨得更红。鼻梁挺直；棕色眉毛浓浓一笔。一种……发怵的、困惑的……怎么说呢？……神情。嗯，西尔维娅是浅色头发；克里斯托弗黑发夹银丝，肤色却白。……见鬼：这少年比克里斯托弗在他这个年纪、更早的时候，还招人喜欢。……克里斯托弗当年在格罗比的家学室门口晃来晃去，对着波动的数学理论发怔。他，马克，那时就容不下他，其实也容不下别的任何一个孩子。还有妹妹埃菲——*天生*就是当副牧师太太的料。……发怔！就是这个词！……就是那个闹人的女人、父亲的第二任妻子——那位圣徒！——把爱发怔的品系引进了蒂金斯家的血统。……这是克里斯托弗的孩子，带着全套圣徒的品系。克里斯托弗天生该在一宗肥缺上做乡区主任牧师，除了星期六下午，成天写微积分的论著。享着圣洁的大名。可他这个没当成，那个也没捞着。他做了个旧家具商，在正人君子的鼻孔里熏出一股臭味。……老天爷行事，神秘莫测。少年这时开口了：

「那棵树……那棵大树。……它遮得窗户发黑。……」

马克心里说了声：「哦嗬！」格罗比大树是蒂金斯家的象征。格罗比方圆三十英里内，人们都在格罗比大树前立婚誓。别的骑区的人说，格罗比树与格罗比井，一高一深，恰相等同。克利夫兰的村民们真要喝到想入非非的醉处，便会宣称——你若否认，他们就揍你——格罗比大树高 365 英尺，格罗比井深 365 英尺。一年一天，一英尺。……逢到特殊的日子——具体什么日子，他懒得去记——人们会来请求准许在枝上挂些布片之类。克里斯托弗说过，对圣女贞德的主要控罪之一，便是她同东雷米村里别的姑娘们曾在一株雪松的枝上挂过布片和小饰物。给仙女的供品。……克里斯托弗顶看重那棵树。他是一头浪漫的蠢驴。多半他对那棵树的看重，超过格罗比的任何别的东西。他若觉得那房子碍了树的势，他能把房子拆了。

===Original===
Young Mark was bleating, positively bleating:

“The Italians have a proverb.﻿ ﻿… He who lets a tree overhang his house invites a daily call from the doctor.﻿ ﻿… I agree myself.﻿ ﻿… In principle, of course.﻿ ﻿…”

Well, that was that! Sylvia, then, was proposing to threaten to ask to have Groby Great Tree cut down. Only to threaten to ask. But that would be enough to agonize the miserable Christopher. You couldn’t cut down Groby Great Tree. But the thought that the tree was under the guardianship of unsympathetic people would be enough to drive Christopher almost dotty﻿—for years and years.

“Mrs. de Bray Pape,” the boy was stammering, “is extremely keen on the tree’s being﻿ ﻿… I agree in principle.﻿ ﻿… My mother wished you to see that﻿—oh, in modern days﻿—a house is practically unlettable if﻿ ﻿… So she got Mrs. de Bray Pape﻿ ﻿… She hasn’t had the courage though she swore she had.﻿ ﻿…”

He continued to stammer. Then he started and stopped, crimson. A woman’s voice had called:

“Mr. Tietjens.﻿ ﻿… Mr. Mark.﻿ ﻿… Hi﻿ ﻿… hup!”

===Chinese===
小马克正咩咩地哀诉着，千真万确是咩咩地：

「意大利人有句谚语。……谁容得树荫罩住自家的屋顶，就是天天邀医生登门。……我个人是赞成的。……当然，原则上赞成。……」

好吧，原来如此！这么说，西尔维娅是打算放出风声，要要求砍掉格罗比大树。不过是摆出要求的架势罢了。可这就足以把那个可怜的克里斯托弗折磨个半死。格罗比大树是砍不得的。可只要一想到这树落在一伙毫无怜惜之心的人手里看管着，就够把克里斯托弗逼得几乎发疯——一年又一年地疯下去。

「德布雷·佩普夫人，」少年结结巴巴地说，「极其希望那棵树被……我原则上赞成。……我母亲想让您明白——噢，如今这年头——一座宅子要是……实际上是租不出去的。所以她找来了德布雷·佩普夫人……她一直没敢真下手，虽然她赌咒发誓说敢。……」

他继续结巴。随即他一惊，停住，满面绯红。一个女人的嗓音喊道：

「蒂金斯先生。……马克先生。……嗨……驾！」

===Original===
A small woman, all in white, white breeches, white coat, white wide-awake, was slipping down from a tall bay with a white star on the forehead﻿—a bay with large nostrils and an intelligent head. She waved her hand obviously at the boy and then caressed the horse’s nostrils. Obviously at the boy﻿ ﻿… for it was obviously unlikely that Mark Senior would know a woman who could make a sound like “Hi, hup!” to attract his attention.

Lord Fittleworth, in a square, hard hat, sat on an immense, coffin-headed dapple-grey. He had bristling, close-cropped moustaches and sat like a limpet. He waved his crop in the direction of Mark and went on talking to Gunning, who was at his stirrup. The coffin-headed beast started forward and reared a foot or so; a wild, brazen, yelping sound had disturbed it. The boy was more and more scarlet and, as emotion grew on him, more and more like Christopher on that beastly day.﻿ ﻿… Christopher with a piece of furniture under his arm, in Marie Léonie’s room, his eyes goggling out at the foot of the bed.

Mark swore painfully to himself. He hated to be reminded of that day. Now this lad and that infernal bugle that the younger children of Cramp had got hold of from their bugler-brother had put it back damnably in his mind. It went on. At intervals. One child had a try, then another. Obviously then Cramp the eldest took it. It blared out.﻿ ﻿… *Ta﻿ ﻿… Ta﻿ ﻿… Ta.﻿ ﻿… Ta﻿ ﻿… ti﻿ ﻿… ta-ta-ti﻿ ﻿… Ta.﻿ ﻿…* The Last Post. The bloody infernal Last Post.﻿ ﻿… Well, Christopher, as that day Mark had predicted, had got himself, with his raw sensibilities, into a pretty bloody infernal mess while some drunken ass had played the Last Post under the window.﻿ ﻿… Mark meant that whilst that farewell was being played he had had that foresight. And he hated the bugle for reminding him of it. He hated it more than he had imagined. He could not have imagined himself using profanity even to himself. He must have been profoundly moved. Deucedly and profoundly moved at that beastly noise. It had come over the day like a disaster. He saw every detail of Marie Léonie’s room as it had been on that day. There was, on the marble mantelshelf, under an immense engraving of the Sistine Madonna, a feeding-cup over a night-light in which Marie Léonie had been keeping some sort of pap warm for him.﻿ ﻿… Probably the last food to which he had ever helped himself.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
一个小巧的女人，通身雪白，白马裤、白外套、白色宽檐软帽，正从一匹高大的栗色马上溜下来——马额头上一颗白星，鼻孔开张，一颗伶俐的头。她朝少年明显地挥了挥手，然后抚摸马鼻孔。明显是朝少年……因为老马克显然不可能认识一个会用「嗨，驾！」这种声音来招他注意的女人。

菲特尔沃思勋爵戴一顶方硬的猎帽，骑一匹高大的棺材头灰斑马。他留两撇簇硬的板刷胡，像颗帽贝似的吸坐在鞍上。他朝马克的方向挥了挥马鞭，继续同贴在他马镫边的冈宁说话。那匹棺材头牲口猛地向前一蹿，人立起来尺把高；一阵狂野刺耳、如犬吠般的铜响惊了它。少年越来越红，情绪一浪高过一浪，人也越来越像那个可憎日子的克里斯托弗。……克里斯托弗腋下夹着一件家具，在玛丽·莱昂妮的房间里，站在床脚，眼睛鼓鼓地瞪出来。

马克忍着痛楚在心里骂了句粗话。他恨人提起那一天。如今这少年，还有克兰普家的小孩子们从他们号手哥哥那里搞来的那支混账军号，把那一天可憎地塞回了他脑子里。号还在响。一阵，又一阵。一个孩子试吹一气，又换一个。后来显然是克兰普家老大接了过去。号轰然响起。……*嗒……嗒……嗒。……嗒……嘀……嗒嗒嘀……嗒。……*熄灯号。混账邪门的熄灯号。……好吧，克里斯托弗，正如马克那天所预言的，当窗外某个蠢驴醉鬼吹着熄灯号的时候，凭着他那副易痛的嫩神经，把自己陷进了一摊混账邪门的烂泥。……马克的意思是，那支送别的号声吹响时，他早有这份先见。他恨这支号，恨它让他想起这个。他恨得超出了自己的想象。他本无法想象自己竟会对自己咒天骂地。他一定是被深深震动了。被那可憎的噪音震得邪门而深沉地动了。它像一场灾祸似的罩在那个日子上。他看见玛丽·莱昂妮的房间当日的每一处细节，历历如在目前。大理石壁炉台上，在一幅巨大的西斯廷圣母雕版画下面，一只喂食杯搁在长明的小夜灯上，玛丽·莱昂妮原是在里面给他温着某种面糊。……多半是他此生亲手取用的最后一份吃食。……

### V / 第五章

===Original===
But no﻿ ﻿… that must have been about twelve, or earlier or later, on that infernal day. In any case he could not remember any subsequent meal he had had then; but he remembered an almost infinitely long period of intense vexation. Of mortification in so far as he could accuse himself of ever having felt mortified. He could still remember the fierce intaking of his breath through his nostrils that had come when Christopher had announced what had seemed to him then his ruinous intentions.﻿ ﻿… It had not been till probably four in the morning that Lord Wolstonmark had rung him, Mark, up to ask him to countermand the transport that was to have gone out from Harwich.﻿ ﻿… At four in the morning, the idiotic brutes.﻿—His substitute had disappeared in the rejoicings in the sy﻿—﻿—, and Lord Wolstonmark had wanted to know what code they used for Harwich because the transport must at all costs be stopped. There was going to be no advance into Germany.﻿ ﻿… He had never spoken after that!

His brother was done for; the country finished; he was as good as down and out, as the phrase was, himself. In his deep mortification﻿—yes, mortification!﻿—he had said to Christopher that morning﻿—the 11th November, 1918﻿—that he would never speak to him again. He hadn’t at that moment meant to say that he would never speak to Christopher at all again﻿—merely that he was never going to speak to him about affairs﻿—the affairs of Groby! Christopher might take that immense, far-spreading, grey bothersome house and the tree and the well and the moors and all the John Peel outfit. Or he might leave them. He, Mark, was never going to speak about the matter any more.

He remembered thinking that Christopher might have taken him to mean that he intended to withdraw, for what it was worth, the light of his countenance from the Christopher Tietjens ménage. Nothing had been further from his thoughts. He had a soft corner in his heart for Valentine Wannop. He had had it ever since sitting, feeling like a fool, in the anteroom of the War Office, beside her﻿—gnawing at the handle of his umbrella. But, then, he had recommended her to become Christopher’s mistress: he had, at any rate, begged her to look after his mutton chops and his buttons. So that it wasn’t likely that when, a year or so later, Christopher announced that he really was at last going to take up with the young woman and to chance what came of it﻿—it wasn’t likely that he intended to dissociate himself from the two of them.

===Chinese===
可是，不对……那准是十二点钟光景，或早或晚，在那倒霉透顶的一天。反正那之后他可曾吃过哪顿饭，一概想不起来了；可他记得一段几乎无穷无尽的、盛怒恼极的时光。说是羞恼也使得——倘若他肯承认自己有过羞恼的话。克里斯托弗宣布了那在他听来足以毁家灭业的打算时，他猛然从鼻孔里倒抽的那一口冷气，至今还感觉得到。……多半要到凌晨四点，沃尔斯顿马克勋爵才打电话给他——给马克——要他撤销那班本该从哈里奇启运的运输船队。……凌晨四点，这帮蠢东西！——他的替班早已在街——上的欢庆人潮里没了影儿，沃尔斯顿马克勋爵急要知道他们同哈里奇之间用哪套电码，因为那班运输船队说什么也必须拦下。不进占德国了。……此后他再没发过一言！

他兄弟算是完了；国家也完了；他自己，照俗话说，也同垮台败落差不多了。在深深的羞恼里——对，羞恼！——那天早晨——1918 年 11 月 11 日——他对克里斯托弗说，他再也不跟他说话了。那一刻他本无意说从此压根儿不理克里斯托弗——只是说，再也不跟他谈事务——格罗比的事务！那座庞大铺展、灰扑扑、招人嫌的大宅，连同那棵树、那口井、那片荒原，还有约翰·皮尔（John Peel，猎狐歌里的猎人）式全套行头，克里斯托弗要便拿去。不要便丢开。他马克，这桩事再不提了。

他记得当时想过，克里斯托弗兴许会把这话领会成：他打算收回——不论值不值几个钱——他对克里斯托弗·蒂金斯那个小家（ménage）的一点点眷顾之色。他心里离这念头十万八千里。他对瓦伦丁·万诺普心坎里存着一块软处。打从那天在陆军部前厅里坐在她身旁——啃着雨伞伞柄——自觉活像个傻子的时候起，就有了。可是话又说回来，当初是他劝她去做克里斯托弗的情妇的：至少，他恳求过她照管他的羊排和纽扣。所以一年多以后，克里斯托弗宣布当真要同这位姑娘同居、后果悉听天命的时候——他不至于存心同他们两个撇清干系。

===Original===
The idea had worried him so much that he had written a rough note﻿—the last time that his hand had ever held a pen﻿—to Christopher. He had said that a brother’s backing was not of great use to a woman, but in the special circumstances of the case, he being Tietjens of Groby for what it was worth, and Lady Tietjens﻿—Marie Léonie﻿—being perfectly willing to be seen on all occasions with Valentine and her man, it might be worth something, at any rate with tenantry and suchlike.

Well, he hadn’t gone back on that!

But once the idea of retiring, not only from the Office but the whole world, had come into his head it had grown and grown, on top of his mortification and his weariness. Because he could not conceal from himself that he was weary to death﻿—of the Office, of the nation, of the world and people.﻿ ﻿… People﻿ ﻿… he was tired of them, and of the streets, and the grass, and the sky and the moors. He had done his job.﻿—That was before Wolstonmark had telephoned, and he still thought that he had done his job of getting things here and there about the world to some purpose.

===Chinese===
这个念头把他搅得不安，竟至提笔写了一封潦草的短简——那是他的手此生最后一次握笔——给克里斯托弗。信里说，做兄长的替女人撑腰，用处不大；但本案情形特殊，他好歹是格罗比的蒂金斯，而蒂金斯夫人——玛丽·莱昂妮——又十分情愿在任何场合同瓦伦丁和她的男人一同露面，这兴许就值几个钱了——至少对佃户之流是如此。

嗯，这个他可没有食言！

可是一旦退隐的念头——不只是退出部里，而是退出整个世界——钻进了脑袋，它便在羞恼与倦怠之上越长越大。因为他瞒不过自己：他已倦得要死——倦这部里，倦这国家，倦这世界与世人。……世人……他腻烦他们了，也腻烦街道、青草、天空和荒原。他的差事已经办完了。——那是在沃尔斯顿马克来电话之前；他至今仍认为，把世上各处的物资源源送到该去的地方，这份差事他办得颇有成效。

===Original===
A man is in the world to do his duty by his nation and his family.﻿ ﻿… By his own people first. Well, he had to acknowledge that he had let his own people down pretty badly﻿—beginning with Christopher. Chiefly Christopher. But that reacted on the tenantry.

He had always been tired of the tenantry and Groby. He had been born tired of them. That happens. It happens particularly in old and prominent families. It was odd that Groby and the whole Groby business should so bore him; he supposed he had been born with some kink. All the Tietjenses were born with some sort of kink. It came from the solitude maybe, on the moors, the hard climate, the rough neighbours﻿—possibly even from the fact that Groby Great Tree overshadowed the house. You could not look out of the schoolroom windows at all for its great, ragged trunk, and all the children’s wing was darkened by its branches. Black!﻿ ﻿… funeral plumes! The Hapsburgs were said to hate their palaces﻿—that was no doubt why so many of them, beginning with Juan Ort, had come muckers. At any rate, they had chucked the royalty business.

And at a very early age he had decided that he would chuck the country-gentleman business. He didn’t see that he was the one to bother with those confounded, hardheaded beggars or with those confounded windswept moors and wet valley bottoms. One owed the blighters a duty, but one did not have to live among them or see that they aired their bedrooms. It had been mostly swank that, always; since the Corn Laws, it had been almost entirely swank. Still, it is obvious that a landlord owes something to the estate from which he and his fathers have drawn their incomes for generations and generations.

===Chinese===
一个男人活在世上，是要对国家与家族尽本分。……首先对自家人。唉，他不得不承认，他把自家人辜负得够呛——从克里斯托弗起头。主要是克里斯托弗。可这也连累到佃户们。

他对佃户和格罗比从来就厌倦。他生下来就厌倦他们。这种事是有的。在古老显赫的门第里尤其常有。说来也怪，格罗比和格罗比整个营生竟这样叫他生厌；他想，自己怕是生来就带着点儿毛病。蒂金斯家的人生来都带点儿毛病。也许是荒原上的孤寂、严酷的气候、粗野的乡邻造成的——说不定还因为格罗比大树荫蔽了宅子。从书房的窗子望出去，满眼都是它那粗砺嶙峋的巨干，儿童住的那一翼全让枝桠罩得暗沉沉的。黑压压！……丧仪上的黑羽！据说哈布斯堡家就恨他们的宫殿——无疑正因如此，他们家那么多人、打胡安·奥特（Juan Ort）起，一个个都栽了大跟头。反正，他们把王室这行当撂了。

而早在很小的时候，他就拿定主意，连乡绅这行当也要撂了。他不明白凭什么该由他来操心那帮精明难缠的老粗，操心那任风狂吹的荒原和湿漉漉的谷底。对这些家伙是欠着一份义务的，可并不非得住在他们中间，也并不非得查看着他们给卧室开窗透气。这一套从来大半是摆阔；打谷物法（Corn Laws）以后，就几乎全是摆阔了。不过，一个地主，对这座他本人和他祖上世世代代支取进项的庄园，总归欠着一份情分，这显而易见。

===Original===
Well, he had never intended to do it, because he had been born tired of it. He liked racing and talking about racing to fellows who liked racing. He had intended to do that to the end.

He hadn’t been able to.

He had intended to go on living between the Office, his chambers, Marie Léonie’s and weekends with racehorse owners of good family until his eyes closed.﻿ ﻿… Of course God disposes in the end, even of the Tietjenses of Groby! He had intended to give over Groby, on the death of his father, to whichever of his brothers had heirs and seemed likely to run the estate well. That would have been quite satisfactory. Ted, his next brother, had had his head screwed on all right. If he had had children he would have filled the bill. So would the next brother.﻿ ﻿… But neither of them had had children and both had managed to get killed in Gallipoli. Even sister Mary, who was actually next to him and a *maîtresse femme* if ever there was one, had managed to get killed as a Red Cross matron. *She* would have run Groby well enough﻿—the great, blowsy, grey woman with a bit of a moustache.

===Chinese===
唉，这份情分他从来没打算还，因为他生来就腻烦它。他喜欢赛马，喜欢跟喜欢赛马的人聊赛马。他本打算就这样过到头。

他没能如愿。

他本打算就这样过下去：部里、自己的寓所、玛丽·莱昂妮那儿，三处轮着住，周末去出身高门的赛马马主人家做客，直到阖眼为止。……当然，到头来总是上帝说了算，连格罗比的蒂金斯家也逃不脱！他本打算等父亲一死，就把格罗比交给兄弟中哪一个有后嗣、又似乎能把庄园整治好的。那样便皆大欢喜。大弟特德（Ted）脑子是清楚的。他要有孩子，就正合适。再下一个兄弟也成。……可两个都没有孩子，还都在加利波利（Gallipoli）送了命。连妹妹玛丽——论次序其实就紧挨着他了——那样一个不折不扣的厉害女人（maîtresse femme）——都当红十字护士长送了命。她倒本能把格罗比管得不坏——那高大、蓬乱粗壮、灰扑扑、唇上微髭的女人。

===Original===
Thus God had let him down with a bump on Christopher.﻿ ﻿… Well, Christopher would have run Groby well enough. But he wouldn’t. Wouldn’t own a yard of Groby land; wouldn’t touch a penny of Groby money. He was suffering for it now.

They were both, in effect, suffering, for Mark could not see what was to become of either Christopher or the estate.

Until his father’s death Mark had bothered precious little about the fellow. He was by fourteen years the younger: there had been ten children altogether, three of his own mother’s children having died young and one having been soft. So Christopher had been still a baby when Mark had left Groby for good﻿—for good except for visits when he had brought his umbrella and seen Christopher mooning at the schoolroom door or in his own mother’s sitting-room. So he had hardly seen the boy.

And at Christopher’s wedding he had definitely decided that he would not see him again﻿—a mug who had got trepanned into marrying a whore. He wished his brother no ill, but the thought of him made Mark sickish. And then, for years, he had heard the worst possible rumours about Christopher. In a way they had rather consoled Mark. God knows, he cared little enough about the Tietjens family﻿—particularly for the children by that soft saint. But he would rather have any brother of his be a wrong un than a mug.

===Chinese===
就这样，上帝兜头一记闷棍，把一切砸到了克里斯托弗头上。……唉，克里斯托弗本会把格罗比管得不坏的。可他不干。格罗比一寸地也不肯要；格罗比一个子儿也不肯碰。他如今正在受这个罪。

说起来，兄弟俩都在受罪，因为马克看不出，克里斯托弗和这座庄园，两下里各自能有个什么下场。

直到父亲去世，马克对这小子难得操过一份心。克里斯托弗比他小十四岁：兄弟姐妹一共十个，他自己生母所出的孩子里三个早夭、一个先天痴傻。所以马克永别格罗比的时候——所谓永别，除却几回撑着伞上门探望，看见克里斯托弗在书房门口发怔，或在他生母的起居室里出神——克里斯托弗还是个奶娃娃。所以这孩子他几乎没见过。

而在克里斯托弗的婚礼上，他更拿定了主意从此不再见他——一个被人诓去娶了婊子的冤大头。他并无意叫兄弟遭殃，可一想起来就恶心。此后多年，他听到的关于克里斯托弗的流言，一个比一个难听。这些话倒多少宽慰了马克。天知道，他对蒂金斯家的兴衰本就没多少牵挂——对那位痴傻圣徒所出的子女尤其如此。不过自家兄弟，他宁可当个恶棍，也强过当冤大头。

===Original===
Then gradually, from the gossip that went abroad, he had come to think that Christopher was a very bad wrong un indeed. He could account for it easily enough. Christopher had a soft streak, and what a woman can do to deteriorate a fellow with a soft streak is beyond belief. And the woman Christopher had got hold of﻿—who had got hold of him﻿—passed belief too. Mark did not hold any great opinion of women at all; if they were a little plump, healthy, a little loyal and not noticeable in their dress that was enough for him.﻿ ﻿… But Sylvia was as thin as an eel, as full of vice as a mare that’s a wrong un, completely disloyal and dressed like any Paris cocotte. Christopher, as he saw it, had had to keep that harlot to the tune of six or seven thousand a year, in a society of all wrong uns too﻿—and on an income of at most two.﻿ ﻿… Plenty for a younger son. But naturally he had had to go wrong to get the money.

So it had seemed to him﻿ ﻿… and it had seemed to matter precious little. He gave a thought to his brother perhaps twice a year. But then one day﻿—just after the two brothers had been killed﻿—their father had come up from Groby to say to Mark at the Club:

“Has it occurred to you that, since those two boys are killed, that fellow Christopher is practically heir to Groby? You have no legitimate children, have you?” Mark replied that he hadn’t any bastards either, and that he was certainly not going to marry.

===Chinese===
后来渐渐地，从外面流传的闲话里，他竟认定克里斯托弗实在是个坏透了的恶棍。这他不难解释。克里斯托弗骨子里有一股软性子，而一个女人能把软性子的男人糟践到什么地步，是难以置信的。克里斯托弗沾上的那个女人——或者说缠住克里斯托弗的那个女人——也同样令人难以置信。马克对女人本就不抱多大指望；只要她们略略丰腴、健康、有点忠心、穿得不惹眼，他就满足了。……可西尔维娅瘦得像条鳗鱼，坏得像匹劣马，毫无忠心可言，穿得同巴黎的野鸡一个样。在马克看来，克里斯托弗不得不拿一年六七千镑供养那娼妇——还是在一圈人人皆是恶棍的社会里——而自己的进项至多两千。……对一个小儿子来说，这本尽够阔绰。可他自然只好走邪路去弄钱。

事情在他看来便是如此……而这似乎也无足轻重。他一年里想起这位兄弟，大约也就两回。可后来有一天——就在两个弟弟阵亡之后不久——他们父亲从格罗比上来，在俱乐部对马克说：

「你可曾想到，那两个孩子既然阵亡了，克里斯托弗那小子实际上就是格罗比的继承人了？你没有嫡出的子女吧？」马克答道，他连私生子也没有一个，而且肯定不打算结婚。

===Original===
At that date it had seemed to him certain that he was not going to marry Papist Marie Léonie Riotor, and certainly he was not going to marry anyone else. So Christopher﻿—or at any rate Christopher’s heir﻿—must surely come in to Groby. It had not really, hitherto, occurred to him. But when it was thus put forcibly into his mind he saw instantly that it upset the whole scheme of his life. As he saw Christopher then, the fellow was the last person in the world to have the charge of Groby﻿—for you had to regard that as to some extent a cure of souls. And he, himself, would not be much better. He was hopelessly out of touch with the estate, and, even though his father’s land-steward was a quite efficient fellow, he himself at that date was so hopelessly immersed in the affairs of the then-war that he would hardly have a moment of time to learn anything about the property.

There was, therefore, a breakdown in his scheme of life. That was already a pretty shaking sort of affair. Mark was accustomed to regard himself as master of his fate﻿—as being so limited in his ambitions and so entrenched behind his habits and his wealth that, if circumstances need not of necessity bend to his will, fate could hardly touch him.

And it was one thing for a Tietjens younger son to be a bold sort of lawbreaker﻿—or at any rate that he should be contemptuous of restraint. It was quite another that the heir to Groby should be a soft sort of bad hat whose distasteful bunglings led his reputation to stink in the nostrils of all his own class. If a younger son can be said to have a class!﻿ ﻿… At any rate in the class to which his father and eldest brother belonged. Tietjens was said to have sold his wife to her cousin the Duke at so contemptible a price that he was obviously penniless even after that transaction. He had sold her to other rich men﻿—to bank managers, for instance. Yet even after that he was reduced to giving stumer cheques. If a man sold his soul to the devil he should at least insist on a good price. Similar transactions were said to distinguish the social set in which that bitch moved﻿—but most of the men who, according to Ruggles, sold their wives to members of the government obtained millions by governmental financial tips﻿—or peerages. Not infrequently they obtained both peerages and millions. But Christopher was such a confounded ass that he had got neither the one nor the other. His cheques were turned down for twopences. And he was such a bungler that he must needs get with child the daughter of their father’s oldest friend, and let the fact be known to the whole world.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
在那时看来，他是肯定不会娶教皇派（Papist，贬称）玛丽·莱昂妮·里奥托的，也肯定不会另娶旁人。所以克里斯托弗——至少克里斯托弗的继承人——必将入主格罗比。这一点此前他实在没有想到过。可这话硬生生塞进脑子之后，他立刻看出，他一生的盘算被搅了个底朝天。照他当时对克里斯托弗的看法，那小子是世上最不该执掌格罗比的人——因为那差事多多少少要算一份牧灵之职。而他自己也好不到哪儿去。他同庄园隔膜得不可救药；即便父亲留下的土地管事相当能干，他本人当时又正不可救药地埋在战事里，几乎匀不出一刻工夫来学一点产业上的事。

于是，他的人生盘算塌了一个大窟窿。这本身已是够吓人的事。马克一向惯于把自己看作自家命运的主人——野心如此有限，又如此深沟高垒地躲在习惯与财富后面，纵使世事未必都屈从他的意志，命运也几乎奈何他不得。

蒂金斯家的小儿子当个胆大包天的犯法之徒——或者说，至少蔑视一切约束——是一回事；格罗比的继承人是个软绵绵的坏蛋，他那叫人作呕的蠢行闹得自己在整个阶层的鼻孔里臭气熏天，却完全是另一回事。小儿子若是说得上有什么所属阶层的话！……至少在他父亲和长兄所属的那个阶层里是如此。据说蒂金斯把老婆卖给了她那位公爵表亲，价钱贱得叫人不齿，以致那桩交易做完他显然还是身无分文。他还把她卖过别的阔人——比如银行经理们。可即便那样之后，他还落得开空头支票。一个人把灵魂卖给魔鬼，至少该坚持卖个好价钱。据说那母狗来往的那个圈子，正以此类交易著称——而且照拉格尔斯的说法，把老婆卖给政府要员的大多数人，都靠政府的财经内线发了百万之财——或者得了封爵。既得封爵又发百万之财的也不在少数。可克里斯托弗偏偏蠢得要命，一样也没捞着。他的支票连两个便士的金额都遭拒付。他还蠢到偏偏叫他们父亲至交的女儿大了肚子，还让这事传遍全世界。……

===Original===
This information he had from Ruggles﻿—and it killed their father. Well, he, Mark, was absolutely to blame: that was that. But﻿—infinitely worse﻿—it had made Christopher absolutely determined not to accept a single penny of the money that had become Mark’s and that had been his father’s. And Christopher was as obstinate as a hog. For that Mark did not blame him. It was a Tietjens job to be as obstinate as a hog.

He couldn’t, however, disabuse his mind of the idea that Christopher’s refusal of Groby and all that came from Groby was as much a manifestation of the confounded saintliness that he got from his soft mother as of a spirit of resentment. Christopher *wanted* to rid himself of his great possessions. The fact that his father and brother had believed him to be what Marie Léonie would have called *maquereau* and had thus insulted him he had merely grasped at with eagerness as an excuse. He wanted to be out of the world. That was it. He wanted to be out of a disgustingly inefficient and venial world, just as he, Mark, also wanted to be out of a world that he found almost more fusionless and dishonest than Christopher found it.

At any rate, at the first word that they had had about the heirship to Groby after their father’s death, Christopher had declared that he, Mark, might take his money to the devil and the ownership of Groby with it. He proposed never to forgive either his father or Mark. He had only consented to take Mark by the hand at the urgent solicitation of Valentine Wannop.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
这些消息他是从拉格尔斯那儿听来的——而这些话要了他们父亲的命。唉，这全怪他马克，没什么可说的。可是——糟糕得没有止境的是——这叫克里斯托弗铁了心：凡是成了马克名下、原属父亲的钱，一个便士也不肯领受。而克里斯托弗倔得像头猪。这一点马克倒不怪他。倔得像头猪，正是蒂金斯家的本色。

然而有一个念头他总抛不开：克里斯托弗拒受格罗比和格罗比名下的一切，既是积怨的发作，也同样是他从那痴傻母亲身上得来的、那股可恶的圣徒气的发作。克里斯托弗巴不得甩脱他偌大的产业。父亲和兄长竟相信他是玛丽·莱昂妮口中的龟公（maquereau，法语骂名）、如此羞辱了他——这个事实，他不过急切地一把抓来当个由头。他要遁出这个世界。就是这么回事。他要遁出一个无能得令人作呕、贿赂公行的世界，正像他马克也想遁出一个世界——一个他发觉比克里斯托弗所见的还没有脊梁、还不诚实的世道。

总之，父亲去世后，关于格罗比继承权的第一句话，克里斯托弗就声言：他马克尽可以带着他的钱下地狱，格罗比的所有权一并带去。他打算永不宽恕父亲，也永不宽恕马克。只是经瓦伦丁·万诺普苦苦相劝，他才肯同马克握一握手。……

===Original===
That had been the most dreadful moment of Mark’s life. The country was, even then, going to the devil; his brother proposed to starve himself; Groby, by his brother’s wish, was to fall into the hands of that bitch.﻿ ﻿… And the country went further and further towards the devil, and his brother starved worse and worse﻿ ﻿… and as for Groby﻿ ﻿…

The boy who practically owned Groby had, at the first sound of the voice of the woman who wore white riding-kit and called “Hi-hup!”﻿—at the very first sound of her voice the boy had scampered off through the raspberry canes and was now against the hedge, whilst she leaned down over him, laughing, and her horse leaned over behind her. Fittleworth was smiling at them benevolently, and at the same time continuing his conversation with Gunning.﻿ ﻿…

The woman was too old for the boy, who had gone scarlet at the sound of her voice. Sylvia had been too old for Christopher: she had got him on the hop when he had been only a kid.﻿ ﻿… The world went on.

===Chinese===
那是马克一生中最可怕的一刻。国家当时就已在往魔鬼那儿滑；兄弟打算饿自己的肚子；而格罗比，依着兄弟的意思，眼看要落进那母狗手里。……此后国家越滑越近魔鬼，兄弟饿得一天紧似一天……至于格罗比嘛……

那个实际上拥有格罗比的男孩，一听见那穿白色骑装、喝着「嗨——起！」的女人的嗓音——她的嗓音才起头，那男孩就一溜烟钻过覆盆子丛，此刻正贴着树篱，她则俯身向他笑着，她的马在她身后也探过身来。菲特尔沃思正慈和地朝他们微笑，一面继续同冈宁谈话。……

对那男孩来说，这女人年纪太大了——听见她嗓音时男孩涨红了脸。西尔维娅对克里斯托弗来说也曾太老：她拿下他的时候，他还不过是个娃娃。……世界照旧运转。

===Original===
He was nevertheless thankful for the respite. He had to acknowledge to himself that he was not as young as he had been. He had a great deal to think of if he was to get the hang of﻿—he was certainly not going to interfere with﻿—the world, and having to listen to conversations that were mostly moral apophthegms had tired him. He had got too many at too short intervals. If he had spoken he would not have, but, because he did not speak, both the lady who was descended from the Maintenon and that boy had peppered him with moral points of view that all required to be considered, without leaving him enough time to get his breath mentally.

The lady had called them a corrupt and effete aristocracy. They were probably not corrupt, but certainly, regarded as landowners, they were effete﻿—both he and Christopher. They were simply bored at the contemplation of that terrific nuisance﻿—and refusing to perform the duties of their post they refused the emoluments too. He could not remember that, after childhood, he had ever had a penny out of Groby. They would not accept that post; they had taken others.﻿ ﻿… Well this was his, Mark’s, last post.﻿ ﻿… He could have smiled at his grim joke.

Of Christopher he was not so sure. That ass was a terrific sentimentalist. Probably he would have liked to be a great landowner, keeping up the gates on the estate﻿—like Fittleworth, who was a perfect lunatic about gates. He was probably even now jaw-jawing Gunning about them, smacking his boot-top with his crop-handle. Yes﻿—keeping up the gates and seeing that the tenants’ land gave so many bushels of wheat to the acre or supported so many sheep the year round.﻿ ﻿… How many sheep would an acre keep all the year round, and how many bushels of wheat, under proper farming, should it give? He, Mark, had not the least idea. Christopher would know﻿—with the difference to be expected of every acre of all the thousand acres of Groby.﻿ ﻿… Yes, Christopher had pored over Groby with the intentness of a mother looking at her baby’s face!

===Chinese===
不过他仍庆幸得了这个喘息。他不得不自认，他不比从前年轻了。要想摸清这个世界的门道——他断不是要插手——需要思量的事多着呢；而听人讲那些满口道德格言的话，实在叫他疲乏。来得太多，隔得太密。倘若他开口说话，本不至于如此；可正因为他不说话，那位自认曼特农家后裔的夫人和那个男孩，便拿种种道德观点劈头盖脸砸他，个个都须掂量，竟不给他喘一口精神气的工夫。

那位夫人说他们是一个腐败没落的贵族阶层。腐败倒未必；但作为土地所有者来看，他们确实没落了——他和克里斯托弗都是。他们只是想起那桩天大的烦心事便腻烦透了——既拒绝履行职守，便也拒领俸禄。他记不得成年以后从格罗比拿过一个便士。那个职位他们不肯就；别的职位他们倒就了。……这不，这就是他马克的最后一岗（last post）了。……他险些为自己的冷笑话笑出声来。

对克里斯托弗他就拿不准了。那头蠢驴是个天大的感伤派。他说不定一直巴望当个大地主，修护着庄园里里外外的门——像菲特尔沃思那样，那人对门简直痴狂。说不定这会儿还在跟冈宁絮叨门的事，拿马鞭柄拍着靴筒。是啊——修护庄园的门，盯紧佃户的地每英亩打多少蒲式耳麦子、全年养得活几只羊。……正经耕作之下，一英亩地全年养得活几只羊、出得了几蒲式耳麦子？他马克毫无概念。克里斯托弗准知道——连格罗比上千英亩里每一英亩各自的差别都说得出。……是啊，克里斯托弗端详格罗比的那股专注劲儿，活像母亲端详婴儿的脸！

===Original===
So that his refusal to take on that stewardship might very well arise from a sort of craving for mortification of the spirit. Old Campion had once said that he believed﻿—he positively believed with shudders﻿—that Christopher desired to live in the spirit of Christ. That had seemed horrible to the General, but Mark did not see that it was horrible, per se.﻿ ﻿… He doubted, however, whether Christ would have refused to manage Groby had it been his job. Christ was a sort of an Englishman, and Englishmen did not, as a rule, refuse to do their jobs.﻿ ﻿… They had not used to. Now, no doubt, they did. It was a Russian sort of trick. He had heard that even before the revolution great Russian nobles would disperse their estates, give their serfs their liberty, put on a hair shirt and sit by the roadside begging.﻿ ﻿… Something like that. Perhaps Christopher was a symptom that the English were changing. He himself was not. He was just lazy and determined﻿—and done with it!

He had not at first been able to believe that Christopher was resolved﻿—with a Yorkshire resolution﻿—to have nothing to do with Groby or his, Mark’s, money. He had, nevertheless, felt a warm admiration for his brother the moment the words had been said. Christopher would take none of his father’s money; he would never forgive either his father or his brother. A proper Yorkshire sentiment, uttered coldly and, as it were, good-humouredly. His eyes, naturally, had goggled, but he had displayed no other emotion.

Nevertheless, Mark had imagined that he might be up to some game. He might be merely meaning to bring Mark to his knees.﻿ ﻿… But how could Mark be more brought to his knees than by offering to give over Groby to his brother? It is true he had kept that up his sleeve whilst his brother had been out in France. After all, there was no sense in offering a fellow who might be going to become food for powder the management of great possessions. He had felt a certain satisfaction in the fact that Christopher *was* going out, though he was confoundedly sorry too. He really admired Christopher for doing it﻿—and he imagined that it might clear some of the smirchiness that must attach to Christopher’s reputation, in spite of what he now knew to be his brother’s complete guiltlessness of the crimes that had been attributed to him. He had, of course, been wrong﻿—he had reckoned without the determined discredit that, after the war was over, the civilian population would contrive to attach to every man who had been to the front as a fighting soldier. After all, that was natural enough. The majority of the male population was civilian, and, once the war was over and there was no more risk, they would bitterly regret that they had not gone. They would take it out of the ex-soldiers all right!

===Chinese===
如此说来，他拒绝承担这份管家的职分，很可能出自一种渴求灵魂自苦的癖性。老坎皮恩有一回说过，他相信——他简直是打着寒颤地相信——克里斯托弗渴望按基督的精神生活。这话在将军听来骇人，但马克看不出那本身有什么骇人之处。……不过他怀疑，倘若管理格罗比是基督的差事，基督会不会推辞。基督也算得上半个英国人，而英国人照例是不推辞差事的。……从前不推。如今自然推了。那是俄国人式的把戏。他听说甚至革命以前，俄国的豪门大贵族就会散尽田产、放农奴自由、穿上苦修毛衣、坐在路边行乞。……诸如此类。或许克里斯托弗正是英格兰人开始转变的一个征候。他自己可不是。他不过是又懒又倔——如此而已！

起初他简直不能相信，克里斯托弗竟拿定了约克郡人的狠劲，要同格罗比、同他马克的钱一刀两断。然而话一出口，他对兄弟顿时生出一份热乎乎的敬佩。父亲的钱他分文不取；父亲和兄长他永不宽恕。一句地道的约克郡气性，说得冷冷的，却又近乎和颜悦色。他的眼珠子自然鼓了一鼓，此外没露半点情绪。

尽管如此，马克仍疑心他在耍什么花招。说不定不过是要逼马克屈膝服软。……可是，除了把格罗比拱手让给兄弟，还有什么更能叫马克屈膝的呢？诚然，兄弟在法国打仗那阵子，这一手他一直揣在袖里没亮。毕竟，对一个眼看要去当炮灰的人，提议请他执掌偌大的产业，实在没道理。克里斯托弗要上前线，他心里自有几分快意，虽然也难过得很。他真心佩服克里斯托弗肯去——他还盘算，这兴许能洗刷克里斯托弗名誉上必然沾着的污点，尽管他如今已经明白，兄弟对横加的那些罪名其实全然无辜。他当然是打错了算盘——他没算到，战后平民百姓会处心积虑地往每一个上过前线打过仗的人身上泼脏水。说到底，这也够自然。男性人口大多数是平民；战争一结束、风险一过去，他们便会懊悔自己没去过。这笔账，他们是一定要从退伍兵身上讨回来的！

===Original===
So that Christopher had rather been additionally discredited than much helped by his services to the country. Sylvia had been able to put it, very reasonably, that Christopher was by nature that idle and dissolute thing, a soldier. That, in times of peace, had helped her a great deal.

Still, Mark had been pleased with his brother, and once Christopher had been invalided back, and had returned to his old-tin saving depot near Ealing, Mark had at once set wheels in motion to get his brother demobilized, so that he might look after Groby. By that time Groby was inhabited by Sylvia, the boy, and Sylvia’s mother. The estate just had to be managed by the land-steward who had served his father, neither Sylvia nor her family having any finger in that; though her mother was able to assure him, Mark, that the estate was doing as well as the Agricultural Committees of grocers and stockjobbers would let it. They insisted on wheat being sown on exposed moors where nothing but heather had a chance, and active moorland sheep being fattened in water-bottoms full of liver fluke. But the land-steward fought them as well as one man could be expected to fight the chosen of a nation of small shopkeepers.﻿ ﻿…

And at that date﻿—the date of Christopher’s return to Ealing﻿—Mark had still imagined that Christopher had really only been holding out for the possession of Groby. He was, therefore, disillusioned rather nastily. He had managed to get Christopher demobilized﻿—without telling him anything about it﻿—by just about the time when the Armistice came along.﻿ ﻿… And then he found that he really had put the fat in the fire!

===Chinese===
于是，克里斯托弗为国服役，非但没捞回多少名声，反倒又添了一层恶名。西尔维娅正好振振有词地说，克里斯托弗天生就是当兵的那种游手好闲、放浪形骸的东西。这句话在太平年月帮了她大忙。

不过马克仍为兄弟高兴；克里斯托弗一伤退回国、回到伊灵（Ealing）附近那座收旧锡的回收站，马克便立刻着手张罗让他复员，好去照管格罗比。那时格罗比住着西尔维娅、那孩子，还有西尔维娅的母亲。庄园只得由侍奉过父亲的土地管事打理，西尔维娅和她娘家谁也插不上手；虽说她母亲倒能向他马克担保，庄园的经营，尽着杂货商和证券经纪人组成的那些农业委员会容许的程度，不算坏。那些人硬要在迎风的荒原上播麦子——那种地方本只有石楠能活——硬要把惯于奔走的荒原羊，圈到满是肝蛭的水洼谷底里催肥。可土地管事同他们斗，也算一个汉子对付一个小店主之国的选民们所能斗到的地步了。……

而在那个时候——克里斯托弗回伊灵的时候——马克还想着，克里斯托弗其实不过是在吊价，非拿到格罗比不可。于是他遭了个不小的闷棍。他设法让克里斯托弗复了员——事先一个字没告诉他——大约就在停战到来的时候。……这才知道，自己真是闯了大祸！

===Original===
He had practically beggared the wretched fellow, who, counting on living on his pay for at least a year longer, had mortgaged his blood-money in order to go into a sort of partnership in an old-furniture business with a confounded American. And, of course, the blood-money was considerably diminished, being an allowance made to demobilized officers computed on the number of their days of service. So he had docked Christopher of two or three hundred pounds. That was the sort of mucky situation into which Christopher might be expected to be got by his well-wishers.﻿ ﻿… There he had been, just before Armistice Day, upon the point of demobilization and without an available penny! It appeared that he had to sell even the few books that Sylvia had left him when she had stripped his house.

That agreeable truth had forced itself on Mark at just the moment when he had been so rotten bad with pneumonia that he might be expected to cash in at any moment. Marie Léonie had indeed, of her own initiative, telephoned to Christopher that he had better come to see his brother if he wanted to meet him on this side of the grave.

They had at once started arguing﻿—or, rather, each had started exposing his views. Christopher stated what he was going to do, and Mark his horror at what Christopher proposed. Mark’s horror came from the fact that Christopher proposed to eschew comfort. An Englishmen’s duty is to secure for himself forever reasonable clothing, a clean shirt a day, a couple of mutton chops grilled without condiments, two floury potatoes, an apple pie with a piece of Stilton and pulled bread, a pint of Club Médoc, a clean room, in the winter a good fire in the grate, a comfortable armchair, a comfortable woman to see that all these were prepared for you, to keep you warm in bed and to brush your bowler and fold your umbrella in the morning. When you had that secure for life, you could do what you liked provided that what you did never endangered that security. What was to be said against that?

===Chinese===
他这等于叫那可怜的家伙破了产。那家伙本指望至少再吃一年军饷，竟把卖命钱（blood-money，按服役天数折算发给复员军官的津贴）抵押了出去，去同一个该死的美国人合伙做旧家具生意。卖命钱自然大大缩了水。于是他等于克扣了克里斯托弗两三百镑。这就是好心的亲友们能给克里斯托弗安排下的腌臜局面。……停战日前夕，他已站在复员的关口，手头竟没有一个用得上的子儿！据说连西尔维娅洗劫他宅子时留下的那几本书，他都不得不卖掉。

这个可人的真相，恰恰赶在马克肺炎病得稀烂、随时可能两腿一蹬的时候，硬挤进了他的脑子。玛丽·莱昂妮确实自作主张给克里斯托弗打了电话：他若想赶在坟墓这一边同兄弟见上一面，最好就来。

两人当即争了起来——或者说，各自开始亮观点。克里斯托弗陈述他要干什么，马克陈述他对克里斯托弗的打算何等骇然。马克之所以骇然，是因为克里斯托弗竟打算弃绝安逸。英国人的本分，是为自己永远确保：像样的衣着；每天一件干净衬衫；两块不加作料烤出来的羊排；两个粉糯的土豆；一份苹果派，就着一角斯蒂尔顿奶酪和手撕面包吃；一品脱俱乐部梅多克（Médoc）红酒；一间干净的屋子；冬天炉膛里一炉旺火；一把舒适的扶手椅；一个舒适的女人，替你把这一切置办妥帖，在床上给你暖被窝，早晨替你刷圆顶礼帽、折好雨伞。这些一旦终身无忧，你爱干什么便干什么，只要所干的事不危及这份安稳。这有什么可指摘的？

===Original===
Christopher had nothing to advance except that he was not going to live in that way. He was not going to live in that way unless he could secure that, or something like it, by his own talents. His only available and at the same time marketable talent was his gift for knowing genuine old furniture. So he was going to make a living out of old furniture. He had had his scheme perfectly matured; he had even secured an American partner, a fellow who had as great a gift for the cajolement of American purchasers of old stuff as he, Christopher, had for its discovery. It was still the war then, but Christopher and his partner, between them, had predicted the American mopping up of the world’s gold supply and the consequent stripping of European houses of old stuff.﻿ ﻿… At that you could make a living.

Other careers, he said, were barred to him. The Department of Statistics, in which he had formerly had a post, had absolutely cold-shouldered him. They were not only adamant, they were also vindictive against civil servants who had become serving soldiers. They took the view that those members of their staffs who had preferred serving were idle and dissolute fellows who had merely taken up arms in order to satisfy their lusts for women. Women had naturally preferred soldiers to civilians; the civilians were now getting back on them. That was natural.

Mark agreed that it was natural. Before he had been interested in his brother as a serving soldier, he had been inclined to consider most soldiers as incompetent over Transport and, in general, nuisances. He agreed, too, that Christopher could not go back to the Department. There he was certainly a marked man. He could possibly have insisted on his rights to be taken back even though his lungs, being by now pretty damaged by exposure, might afford them a pretext for legally refusing him. H.M. Civil Service and Departments have the right to refuse employment to persons likely to become unfit for good. A man who has lost an eye may be refused by any Department because he may lose the other and so become liable for a pension. But, even if Christopher forced himself on the Department, they would have their bad mark against him. He had been too rude to them during the war when they had tried to force him to employ himself in the faking of statistics that the Ministry had coerced the Department into supplying in order to dish the French, who demanded more troops.

===Chinese===
克里斯托弗拿不出别的道理，只说他不打算这样过活。不打算这样过活——除非凭自己的才干挣得这一份、或与此相仿的东西。他唯一拿得出手又卖得开的才干，是辨识真古家具的眼力。所以他打算靠旧家具谋生。他的方案已琢磨得烂熟；甚至连美国合伙人都已找好——那家伙哄诓美国买家买旧货的本事，同克里斯托弗发掘旧货的本事一样大。那时还在打仗，可克里斯托弗同他的合伙人已经料定：美国人将把世界的黄金搜刮一空，欧洲人家里的旧货也将随之被剥个精光。……干这一行，是能糊口的。

他说，别的门路都对他关死了。他先前任职的统计部（Department of Statistics）对他冷若冰霜。他们不但寸步不让，还对投笔从戎的文官存心报复。他们的看法是：宁肯去当兵的那些职员，本是些游手好闲、放浪形骸的家伙，拿枪不过是为了满足对女人的欲念。女人天生爱大兵不爱平民；如今平民要跟他们算账了。这很自然。

马克承认这很自然。早在关切当兵的兄弟之前，他就倾向于认为，大多数军人对「运输」是外行，总的说来是祸害。他也承认，克里斯托弗回不去统计部了。他在那里肯定是个挂了号的人。他本可以力争复职的权利，尽管他的肺经风霜之故已损坏得相当厉害，人家大可拿这个作由头、合法地拒收他。皇家文官系统及各部有权拒绝录用可能永久丧失任职能力的人。瞎了一只眼的人，任何部门都可拒收，因为他可能把另一只也瞎掉，从而有资格领一份养老金。可即便克里斯托弗硬挤回部里，人家记他的那一笔坏账也还在。战时他们曾逼他去干伪造统计数字的营生——上级部里胁迫统计部提供假数字，好坑一坑要求增兵的法国人——那时他对他们太无礼了。

===Original===
With that point of view, Mark found himself entirely in sympathy. His long association with Marie Léonie, his respect for the way in which she had her head screwed on, the constant intimacy with the life and point of view of French individuals of the *petite bourgeoisie* which her gossip had given him﻿—all these things, together with his despair for the future of his own country, had given him a very considerable belief in the destinies and, indeed, in the virtues of the country across the Channel. It would, therefore, have been very distasteful to him that his brother should take pay from an organization that had been employed to deal treacherously with our Allies. It had, indeed, become extremely distasteful to him to take pay himself from a Government that had forced such a course upon the nation, and he would thankfully have resigned from his Office if he had not considered that his services were indispensable to the successful prosecution of the war which was then still proceeding. He wanted to be done with it, but, at the moment, he saw no chance. The war was by then obviously proceeding towards a successful issue. Owing to the military genius of the French, who, by then, had the supreme command, the enemy nations were daily being forced to abandon great stretches of territory. But that only made the calls on Transport the greater, whilst, if we were successfully and unwastefully to occupy the enemy capital, as at that date he imagined that we obviously must, the demand for the provision of Transport must become almost unmeasurable.

Still, that was no argument for the reentry of his brother into the service of the country. As he saw things, public life had become﻿—and must remain for a long period﻿—so demoralized by the members of the then Government, with their devious foreign policies and their intimacies with a class of shady financiers such as had never hitherto had any finger in the English political pie﻿—public life had become so discreditable an affair that the only remedy was for the real governing classes to retire altogether from public pursuits. Things, in short, must become worse before they could grow better. With the dreadful condition of ruin at home and foreign discredit to which the country must almost immediately emerge under the conduct of the Scotch grocers, Frankfurt financiers, Welsh pettifoggers, Midland armament manufacturers and South Country incompetents who during the later years of the war had intrigued themselves into office﻿—with that dreadful condition staring it in the face, the country must return to something like its old standards of North Country common sense and English probity. The old governing class to which he and his belonged might never return to power, but, whatever revolutions took place﻿—and he did not care!﻿—the country must reawaken to the necessity for exacting of whoever might be its governing class some semblance of personal probity and public honouring of pledges. He obviously was out of it, or he would be out of it with the end of the war, for even from his bed he had taken no small part in the directing of affairs at his Office.﻿ ﻿… A state of war obviously favoured the coming to the top of all kinds of devious stormy petrels; that was inevitable and could not be helped. But in normal times a country﻿—every country﻿—was true to itself.

Nevertheless, he was very content that his brother should, in the interim, have no share in affairs. Let him secure his mutton chop, his pint of claret, his woman and his umbrella, and it mattered not into what obscurity he retired. But how was that to be secured? There had seemed to be several ways.

===Chinese===
对这套看法，马克衷心赞同。同玛丽·莱昂妮的长年相守，对她那副精明头脑的敬重，她的闲话带给他的、对法国小资产阶级（petite bourgeoisie）个人生活与观念的长期浸润——凡此种种，加上他对本国前途的绝望，使他对海峡对岸那个国家的国运、乃至其德性，都有了相当坚定的信心。因此，倘使兄弟要从一个曾受雇坑骗盟邦的机构领薪水，他会极不痛快。说真的，他自己从一个把这条路线强加给国家的政府手里领薪水，也早已极不痛快；若非认定自己的效力对当时仍在进行的战争的最后胜利不可或缺，他早就谢天谢地辞了部职。他想就此撒手，可当时看不到半点机会。战事那时显然正走向胜利。由于此时执掌最高统帅权的法国人的军事天才，敌国每日都被迫放弃大片的领土。但这只会使「运输」的担子更重；而且，倘若我们要顺利而无靡费地进占敌国都城——照他当时的想法，我们显然必须进占——对「运输」的需求几乎要变得不可计量。

不过，这仍不成其为让兄弟重返国家公务的理由。照他看，公共生活已被当时政府诸公搞得一塌糊涂——而且势必要继续烂上许久——他们外交路线诡诈，又同一伙此前从不曾沾过英格兰政治锅边的下作金融家过从甚密——公共生活已成了如此不体面的一桩营生，唯一的救治之法，就是真正的统治阶层干脆从公共事务中全体引退。简言之，事情必得先烂透，然后才会好转。国家在苏格兰杂货商、法兰克福金融家、威尔士讼棍、中部军火制造商和南方低能儿的主持下——这伙人在战争后期靠阴谋钻营上了台——眼看马上就要陷入国内崩坏、国外失信的可怕境地；面对这副可怕光景，国家必须回到几分北方人的常识与英格兰人的正直那些老标准上来。他和他的族类所属的旧统治阶层也许永无再掌权之日；可是，无论发生什么革命——他才不在乎！——国家总得重新醒悟：无论当家的是哪个阶层，总得向他们索要一点像样的个人操守、一点公开履约的信誉。他自己显然已经出局，或者说战争一结束便要出局，因为即便卧病在床，他仍在部务的指挥上出过不小的力。……战争状态显然利于各路诡诈的海燕趁乱翻上浪尖；这不可避免，没法子。可到了太平年月，一个国家——每一个国家——总归要还它的本来面目。

尽管如此，在此期间兄弟不沾政务，他是很满意的。由他去挣他的羊排、他的一品脱红酒、他的女人、他的雨伞；退隐到多么湮没无闻的角落，都无关紧要。可这份安稳怎么挣得来呢？当时看，路子倒像有好几条。

===Original===
He was aware, for instance, that Christopher was both a mathematician of no mean order and a Churchman. He might perfectly well take orders, assume the charge of one of the three family livings that Mark had in his gift, and, whilst competently discharging the duties of his cure, pursue whatever are the occupations of a well-cared-for mathematician.

Christopher, however, whilst avowing his predilection for such a life﻿—which, as Mark saw it, was exactly fitted to his asceticism, his softness in general and his private tastes﻿—Christopher admitted that there was an obstacle to his assuming such a cure of souls﻿—an obstacle of an insuperable nature. Mark at once asked him if he were, in fact, living with Miss Wannop. But Christopher answered that he had not seen Miss Wannop since the day of his second proceeding to the front. They had then agreed that they were not the sort of persons to begin a hidden intrigue, and the affair had proceeded no further.
